\displaystyle=\Big|\int_{t_{j-1}}^{t_{j}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{v}^{(j-1)}\times\nabla\boldsymbol{\eta}^{(j)},\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(j)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\mathrm{d}s\Big|

\displaystyle\leq k\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\eta}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

(4.14)

\displaystyle\leq ck\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\eta}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\delta k\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

where in the last step we used the Sobolev embedding.
Similarly, we have for the third term,

\displaystyle|T_{3}|

\displaystyle:=\Big|\int_{t_{j-1}}^{t_{j}}\left\langle\big(\boldsymbol{\eta}^{(j-1)}+\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(j-1)}\big)\times\nabla\boldsymbol{w}(s),\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(j)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\mathrm{d}s\Big|

\displaystyle\leq k\left\|{\boldsymbol{\eta}^{(j-1)}+\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{L}^{4})}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq ck\left\|{\boldsymbol{\eta}^{(j-1)}+\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{W}^{1,4})}^{2}+\delta k\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq ck\left\|{\boldsymbol{\eta}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{H}^{1+\alpha})}^{2}+ck\left\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{H}^{1+\alpha})}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\delta k\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{H}^{1+\alpha})}^{2}+\delta k\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

where in the last step we used the Gagliardo–Nirenberg and the Young inequalities.
For the second term
T_{2}
, we have by using (
4.18
),
Hölder’s and Young’s inequalities

\displaystyle|T_{2}|

\displaystyle:=\Big|\int_{t_{j-1}}^{t_{j}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{v}^{(j-1)}\times(\nabla\boldsymbol{w}(t_{j})-\nabla\boldsymbol{w}(s)),\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(j)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\mathrm{d}s\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\int_{t_{j-1}}^{t_{j}}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{w}(t_{j})-\nabla\boldsymbol{w}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq k^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{L}^{2})}

(4.15)

\displaystyle\leq ck^{3}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{L}^{2})}^{2}+\delta k\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Similarly, for the last term
T_{4}
we have, again by using (
4.18
),

\displaystyle|T_{4}|

\displaystyle:=\Big|\int_{t_{j-1}}^{t_{j}}\left\langle\big(\boldsymbol{w}(t_{j-1})-\boldsymbol{w}(s)\big)\times\nabla\boldsymbol{w}(s),\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(j)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\mathrm{d}s\Big|

\displaystyle\leq k^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{L}^{4})}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{L}^{4})}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq ck^{3}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{H}^{1})}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{H}^{1+\alpha})}^{2}+\delta k\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Altogether, we deduce (
4.10
). To prove (
4.11
) we
write
H_{2}(\boldsymbol{v}^{(j)},\boldsymbol{w},\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(j)})
as

\displaystyle H_{2}(\boldsymbol{v}^{(j)},\boldsymbol{w},\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(j)})

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{j-1}}^{t_{j}}\left\langle|\boldsymbol{v}^{(j-1)}|^{2}\big(\boldsymbol{w}(t_{j})-\boldsymbol{w}(s)\big),\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(j)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{t_{j-1}}^{t_{j}}\left\langle\big(|\boldsymbol{v}^{(j-1)}|^{2}-|\boldsymbol{w}(t_{j-1})|^{2}\big)\boldsymbol{w}(s),\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(j)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{t_{j-1}}^{t_{j}}\left\langle\big(|\boldsymbol{w}(t_{j-1})|^{2}-|\boldsymbol{w}(s)|^{2}\big)\boldsymbol{w}(s),\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(j)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=:S_{1}+S_{2}+S_{3}.

By using Hölder’s and Young’s inequalities, together
with (
4.18
), we deduce

\displaystyle|S_{1}|

\displaystyle:=\Big|\int_{t_{j-1}}^{t_{j}}\left\langle|\boldsymbol{v}^{(j-1)}|^{2}\big(\boldsymbol{w}(t_{j})-\boldsymbol{w}(s)\big),\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(j)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{|\boldsymbol{v}^{(j-1)}||\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(j)}|}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\int_{t_{j-1}}^{t_{j}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}(t_{j})-\boldsymbol{w}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{3}}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq k^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}\left\|{|\boldsymbol{v}^{(j-1)}||\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(j)}|}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{L}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq ck^{3}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{H}^{1})}^{2}+\delta k\left\|{|\boldsymbol{v}^{(j-1)}||\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(j)}|}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

\displaystyle|S_{2}|

\displaystyle:=\Big|\int_{t_{j-1}}^{t_{j}}\left\langle\big(|\boldsymbol{v}^{(j-1)}|^{2}-|\boldsymbol{w}(t_{j-1})|^{2}\big)\boldsymbol{w}(s),\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(j)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\mathrm{d}s\Big|

\displaystyle\leq k\left\|{\boldsymbol{\eta}^{(j-1)}+\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}^{(j-1)}+\boldsymbol{w}(t_{j-1})}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{L}^{6})}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}

\displaystyle\leq ck\left\|{\boldsymbol{\eta}^{(j-1)}+\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}^{(j-1)}+\boldsymbol{w}(t_{j-1})}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{H}^{1})}^{2}+\delta k\left\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2},

and

\displaystyle|S_{3}|

\displaystyle:=\Big|\int_{t_{j-1}}^{t_{j}}\left\langle\big(|\boldsymbol{w}(t_{j-1})|^{2}-|\boldsymbol{w}(s)|^{2}\big)\boldsymbol{w}(s),\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(j)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\mathrm{d}s\Big|

\displaystyle\leq ck^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{L}^{2})}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}(t_{j-1})+\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{L}^{6})}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{L}^{6})}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}

\displaystyle\leq ck^{3}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{L}^{2})}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{H}^{1})}^{4}+\delta k\left\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}.

Altogether, we obtain (
4.11
).

Next, we show (
4.12
).
By estimating the terms
T_{1}
and
T_{2}
differently in (
4.13
), we obtain instead of (
4.14
) and (
4.15
),

\displaystyle\left|{T_{1}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq ck\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}^{2}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\eta}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{3}}^{2}+\delta k\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

\displaystyle\left|{T_{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq ck^{3}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}^{2}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{L}^{3})}^{2}+\delta k\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

The terms
T_{3}
and
T_{4}
are estimated in the same manner as before. Therefore, by the embedding
\mathbb{H}^{1}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{L}^{6}
, we have (
4.12
). This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

For any
t\in[0,T]
, we define
\boldsymbol{\rho}(t):=\boldsymbol{u}(t)-P_{h}\boldsymbol{u}(t)
. It follows
that
\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\rho}(t)=\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}(t)-P_{h}\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}(t)

so that (
4.4
) implies, for
\alpha\in(\frac{1}{2},\alpha_{0})
, we have

(4.16)

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{\rho}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+h\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\rho}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{1+\alpha}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1+\alpha}},

\displaystyle\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\rho}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+h\left\|{\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\rho}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq ch^{1+\alpha}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1+\alpha}}.

For any
j=1,\ldots,n
,
letting
\boldsymbol{\rho}^{(j)}:=\boldsymbol{\rho}(t_{j})
and
\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}:=P_{h}\boldsymbol{u}(t_{j})-\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(j)}
,
we split the error into

(4.17)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{u}(t_{j})-\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(j)}=\boldsymbol{\rho}^{(j)}+\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}.

In the following proof, we will also use the following inequality: If
\boldsymbol{v}\in W^{1,\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{L}^{p})
for some
p\in[1,\infty]
and if
s_{1},s_{2}\in[t_{j-1},t_{j}]
for some
j=1,\ldots,N
such that
s_{1}<s_{2}
, then

(4.18)

\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}(s_{2})-\boldsymbol{v}(s_{1})}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{p}}\leq\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{v}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{p}}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq k\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{L}^{p})}.

We can now prove an
a priori
error estimate for the scheme presented in Algorithm
4.1
. By using interpolation in the temporal variable, one can deduce from the theorem below that an approximate solution
\boldsymbol{u}_{h,k}
can be defined from
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(n)}
such that
\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h,k}-\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathbb{L}^{2})}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h,k}-\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathbb{H}^{1})}
satisfies (
4.19
).

Theorem 4.4
.

Assume that the exact solution
\boldsymbol{u}
of the LLBE exists with regularity given by (
4.1
). Let
\big\{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(j)}\big\}_{j=1}^{N}
be the sequence defined by Algorithm
4.1
.
For any
n=1,\ldots,N
, where
t_{n}\in[0,T]
, we have

(4.19)

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(n)}-\boldsymbol{u}(t_{n})}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\left(k\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(j)}-\boldsymbol{u}(t_{j})}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq c\ell_{h}(h^{\alpha}+k),

where
\ell_{h}
is defined in (
4.5
), and
c
is a constant independent of
h
and
k
(but may depend on
T
). In particular, under the time-step constraint
k=o(\ell_{h}^{-1})
as
h,k\to 0^{+}
, we have

\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(n)}-\boldsymbol{u}(t_{n})}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\left(k\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(j)}-\boldsymbol{u}(t_{j})}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\to 0\;\text{ as }\;h,k\to 0^{+}.

Proof.

For any
\boldsymbol{\phi}_{h}\in\mathbb{V}_{h}
, the exact solution
\boldsymbol{u}
of (
1.1
) satisfies, for any
\boldsymbol{\phi}_{h}\in\mathbb{V}_{h}
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}(t_{j})-\boldsymbol{u}(t_{j-1}),\boldsymbol{\phi}_{h}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\epsilon\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{u}(t_{j})-\nabla\boldsymbol{u}(t_{j-1}),\nabla\boldsymbol{\phi}_{h}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\int_{t_{j-1}}^{t_{j}}\kappa_{1}\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{u}(s),\nabla\boldsymbol{\phi}_{h}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\mathrm{d}s

(4.20)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{t_{j-1}}^{t_{j}}\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}(s)\times\nabla\boldsymbol{u}(s),\nabla\boldsymbol{\phi}_{h}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{t_{j-1}}^{t_{j}}\kappa_{2}\left\langle(1+\mu|\boldsymbol{u}(s)|^{2})\boldsymbol{u}(s),\boldsymbol{\phi}_{h}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\mathrm{d}s=0.

Subtracting (
4.20
) from (
4.7
), noting (
4.17
) and (
4.2
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j-1)},\boldsymbol{\phi}_{h}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle+k\kappa_{1}\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}+\nabla\boldsymbol{\rho}^{(j)},\nabla\boldsymbol{\phi}_{h}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\kappa_{1}\int_{t_{j-1}}^{t_{j}}\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(t_{j})-\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s),\nabla\boldsymbol{\phi}_{h}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle+k\kappa_{2}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)},\boldsymbol{\phi}_{h}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\int_{t_{j-1}}^{t_{j}}\kappa_{2}\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(t_{j})-\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s),\boldsymbol{\phi}_{h}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle+\gamma H_{1}(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(j)},\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon},\boldsymbol{\phi}_{h})+\kappa_{2}\mu H_{2}(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(j)},\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon},\boldsymbol{\phi}_{h})

(4.21)

\displaystyle+k\kappa_{2}\mu\left\langle|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(j-1)}|^{2}\big(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{(j)}\big),\boldsymbol{\phi}_{h}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}=0,

where
H_{1}
and
H_{2}
are defined in Lemma
4.3
.

Putting
\boldsymbol{\phi}_{h}=\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}
in (
4.21
), using the vector identity (
4.8
), and rearranging the terms, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+k\kappa_{1}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+k\kappa_{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\kappa_{2}\mu k\left\|{\big|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(j-1)}\big|\big|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}\big|}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=-k\kappa_{1}\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{\rho}^{(j)},\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}-\int_{t_{j-1}}^{t_{j}}\kappa_{1}\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{u}(t_{j})-\nabla\boldsymbol{u}(s),\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{t_{j-1}}^{t_{j}}\kappa_{2}\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}(t_{j})-\boldsymbol{u}(s),\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\mathrm{d}s-\gamma H_{1}(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(j)},\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)})

\displaystyle\quad+\kappa_{2}\mu H_{2}(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(j)},\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)})-k\kappa_{2}\mu\left\langle|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(j-1)}|^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}{(j)},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left(k\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\rho}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\int_{t_{j-1}}^{t_{j}}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}(t_{j})-\nabla\boldsymbol{u}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)

\displaystyle\quad+c\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\int_{t_{j-1}}^{t_{j}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}(t_{j})-\boldsymbol{u}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\mathrm{d}s+c\left|{H_{1}(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(j)},\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)})}\right|

\displaystyle\quad+c\left|{H_{2}(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(j)},\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)})}\right|+ck\left\|{|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(j-1)}||\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}|}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\rho}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}

(4.22)

\displaystyle=:R_{1}+\cdots+R_{5}.

We now estimate each term on the right-hand side. Let
\delta>0
be given and let
\alpha
satisfies (
2.5
). For
the first term, by (
4.18
) and Young’s inequality (noting the regularity (
4.1
)),

\displaystyle R_{1}

\displaystyle:=c\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left(k\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\rho}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\int_{t_{j-1}}^{t_{j}}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}(t_{j})-\nabla\boldsymbol{u}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left(ck\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\rho}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+ck^{2}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{L}^{2})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\delta k\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+ck\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\rho}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+ck^{3}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{L}^{2})}^{2}

(4.23)

\displaystyle\leq ckh^{2\alpha}+ck^{3}+\delta k\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

where in the last step we used (
4.16
).
For the second term, by Hölder’s and Young’s inequalities, and (
4.18
), we have

\displaystyle R_{2}

\displaystyle:=c\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\int_{t_{j-1}}^{t_{j}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}(t_{j})-\boldsymbol{u}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq ck^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{L}^{2})}\leq\delta k\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+ck^{3}.

For the term
R_{3}
, we invoke Lemma
4.3
(with
\boldsymbol{v}^{(j)}=\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(j)}
,

\boldsymbol{w}=\boldsymbol{u}
,
\boldsymbol{\eta}^{(j)}=\boldsymbol{\rho}^{(j)}
,
and
\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(j)}=\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}
), use the fact that
\boldsymbol{u}
is a strong solution, and apply (
4.16
) to obtain

\displaystyle R_{3}:=c\,\big|H_{1}(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(j)},\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)})\big|

\displaystyle\leq c\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\right)k(h^{2\alpha}+k^{2})+ck\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+ck^{3}

\displaystyle\quad+\delta k\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+4\delta k\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle R_{4}:=c\,\big|H_{2}(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(j)},\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)})\big|

\displaystyle\leq ck\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\right)\left(h^{2(1+\alpha)}+k^{2}\right)+ck\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+ck^{3}\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\right)+2\delta k\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\delta k\left\|{|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(j-1)}||\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}|}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Finally,

\displaystyle R_{5}

\displaystyle:=ck\left\|{|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(j-1)}||\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}|}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\rho}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}\leq ckh^{2(1+\alpha)}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\delta k\left\|{|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(j-1)}||\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}|}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Altogether, we derive from (
4.22
) that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\Big(\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\Big)+\frac{1}{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+k\kappa_{1}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+k\kappa_{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\kappa_{2}\mu k\left\|{\big|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(j-1)}\big|\big|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}\big|}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq ck\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\right)\left(h^{2\alpha}+k^{2}\right)+ck\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\delta k\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+8\delta k\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+2\delta k\left\|{|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(j-1)}||\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}|}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

We now choose
\delta=\frac{1}{10}\min\{\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\kappa_{2}\mu\}
to absorb the last three terms on the right-hand side to the last three terms on the left-hand side. In this manner we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+k\kappa_{1}\Big(\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq ck\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\right)\left(h^{2\alpha}+k^{2}\right)+ck\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq ck\ell_{h}^{2}\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\right)\left(h^{2\alpha}+k^{2}\right)+ck\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

where inverse inequality (
4.6
) was used in the last step.
Summing this over
j=1,\ldots,n
, and using Lemma
4.2
, we deduce that

\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(n)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+k\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq ck\ell_{h}^{2}(h^{2\alpha}+k^{2})+ck\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

where
c
is independent of
n
,
h
, and
k
.
Applying the discrete version of the Gronwall inequality
(
Sugiyama, 1969
, Lemma 1)
, we
deduce that

\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(n)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+k\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq ce^{cT}\ell_{h}(h^{\alpha}+k).

Using (
4.16
)
and the triangle inequality, we obtain the required estimate.
∎

5.
Solution to the
\epsilon
-LLBE

The theoretical rate
O(\ell_{h}(h^{\alpha}+k))
established in (
4.19
) is not optimal in light of the numerical results from Simulation 1 in Section
8
; see Figures
5(a)
and
5(b)
.
This motivates us to consider a regularisation of the LLBE and to investigate a numerical method for discretising the
\epsilon
-LLBE, which serves as a theoretical proxy for the original LLBE. First, we need establish the existence of a solution to the regularised problem (
1.2
) and its uniqueness. In Section
5.2
, we then show convergence of
\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}
to
\boldsymbol{u}
. Throughout this section, let
T>0
be a given number which can be arbitrarily large.

5.1.
Existence and uniqueness

As before, the Faedo–Galerkin method will be used to show the existence of a solution to the
\epsilon
-LLBE.
Let
\{(\boldsymbol{e}_{i},\lambda_{i})\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
be a sequence of eigenpairs of the negative Neumann Laplace operator.
Let
\mathcal{S}_{n}:=\text{span}\{\boldsymbol{e}_{1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{e}_{n}\}
and let

\Pi_{n}
be the
\mathbb{L}^{2}
-projection onto
\mathcal{S}_{n}
. We consider the following
approximation to (
1.2
): Find
\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(\cdot,t)\in\mathcal{S}_{n}

satisfying

(5.1)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}_{n}-\epsilon\Delta\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}_{n}

\displaystyle=\kappa_{1}\Delta\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}_{n}+\gamma\Pi_{n}\bigl(\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}_{n}\times\Delta\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}_{n}\bigr)-\kappa_{2}\Pi_{n}\bigl((1+\mu|\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}_{n}|^{2})\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}_{n}\bigr)

\displaystyle\text{in }(0,T)\times\mathscr{D},

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(0)

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{u}_{0,n}^{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\text{in }\mathscr{D}.

We assume that
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}^{\epsilon}
in (
1.2b
) and
\boldsymbol{u}_{0,n}^{\epsilon}\in\mathcal{S}_{n}
in (
5.1
) are chosen such that the initial data approximations satisfy

(5.2a)

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}^{\epsilon}-\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{s}_{\Delta}}

\displaystyle=0,

(5.2b)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0,n}^{\epsilon}-\boldsymbol{u}_{0}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{s}_{\Delta}}

\displaystyle=0\quad\text{uniformly with respect to $\epsilon\in(0,1)$},

for some
s\in\{1,2\}
to be specified in the estimates.
It follows from (
5.2a
) and (
5.2b
) that

(5.3)

\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0,n}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{s}_{\Delta}}\leq c,\quad\forall\epsilon\in(0,1),\ \forall n\in\mathbb{N}.

Local existence of a solution to (
5.1
) can be shown as in Lemma
3.1
.

We now show some bounds on the local solution
\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}

of (
5.1
), which will imply its global existence, i.e., existence of solution on
[0,T]
for any given
T>0
. We also show that
for each
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, the sequence
\{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}\}
converges as
n\to\infty

and its limit is the solution
\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}
of (
1.2
).

First, we rewrite (
5.1
) in the following equivalent form: for
every
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t),\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle+\epsilon\left\langle\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t),\nabla\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\kappa_{1}\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t),\nabla\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\kappa_{2}\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t),\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

(5.4a)

\displaystyle=\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)\times\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t),\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}-\kappa_{2}\mu\left\langle|\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t),\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}},\quad\forall\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{H}^{1},

(5.4b)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(0)

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}_{0,n}.

The stability of the approximate solution
\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}
in various spaces will be shown in a series of lemmas.

Lemma 5.1
.

Assume that (
5.2a
) and (
5.2b
) hold for
s=1
. For any
\epsilon\in[0,1)
and

n\in\mathbb{N}
,

(5.5)

\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}_{n}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}_{n}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}_{n}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}_{n}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}^{4}\mathrm{d}s\leq c,

where the constant
c
depends on
\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}_{\Delta}}
, but is independent of
n
,
\epsilon
, and
t
.

Proof.

Letting
\boldsymbol{v}=\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)
in (
5.4a
) and
noting
(\boldsymbol{a}\times\boldsymbol{b})\cdot\boldsymbol{b}=0
give

(5.6)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}{}\|\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}_{n}(t)\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}{}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}_{n}(t)\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\kappa_{1}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}_{n}(t)\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\kappa_{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\kappa_{2}\mu\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}^{4}=0.

In a similar fashion, by choosing
\boldsymbol{v}=-\Delta\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}_{n}(t)\in\mathcal{S}_{n}

in (
5.4a
) we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}{}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}_{n}(t)\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}{}\|\Delta\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}_{n}(t)\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\kappa_{1}\|\Delta\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}_{n}(t)\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\kappa_{2}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

(5.7)

\displaystyle=\kappa_{2}\mu\left\langle|\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}_{n}(t)|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}_{n}(t),\Delta\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}_{n}(t)\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}.

Note that by applying integration by parts,

\displaystyle\kappa_{2}\mu\left\langle|\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}_{n}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}_{n},\Delta\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\kappa_{2}\mu\left\langle\nabla\big(|\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}_{n}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}\big),\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle=-2\kappa_{2}\mu\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}\cdot\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\kappa_{2}\mu\left\|{|\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}|\,|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}|}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq 0.

Integrating (
5.6
) and (
5.7
) yields (
5.5
), thus completing the proof of the lemma.
∎

Lemma 5.2
.

Assume that (
5.2a
) and (
5.2b
) hold for
s=1
.
For any
\epsilon\in(0,1)
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the approximate
solution
\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}
satisfies

(5.8)

\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{\epsilon^{\beta}},

where
\beta
is defined by

(5.9)

\displaystyle\beta

\displaystyle:=\min\{2,d\}.

The constant depends on
\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}_{\Delta}}
, but is independent of
n
,
\epsilon
, and
t
.

Proof.

Setting
\boldsymbol{v}=\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)
in (
5.4a
) and applying Young’s inequality give

\displaystyle\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\kappa_{1}\left\langle\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t),\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}-\kappa_{2}\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t),\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)\times\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t),\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad-\kappa_{2}\mu\left\langle|\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t),\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+c\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+4\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+c\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}^{6}+\frac{1}{4}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq c+c\epsilon^{-1}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{L}^{\infty})}^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

where in the last step we used the Sobolev embedding
\mathbb{H}^{1}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{L}^{6}
and (
5.5
). The constant
c
depends on
\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}_{\Delta}}
, but is independent of
n
,
\epsilon
, and
t
.
This implies

\displaystyle\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq c+c\epsilon^{-1}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{L}^{\infty})}^{2}.

It remains to prove that
\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{L}^{\infty})}^{2}\leq\epsilon^{1-\beta}
.
Now, if
d=1
, then

(5.10)

\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{L}^{\infty})}^{2}\lesssim\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{H}^{1})}^{2}\lesssim 1.

For
d=2
, we apply the Sobolev embedding
\mathbb{H}^{3/2}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{L}^{\infty}
, the regularity shift estimate (
A.4
), and Lemma
5.1
to obtain

(5.11)

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{L}^{\infty})}^{2}\lesssim\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{H}^{3/2})}^{2}\lesssim\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{L}^{2})}^{2}+\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{L}^{2})}^{2}\lesssim\epsilon^{-1}.

The same result holds for
d=3
if we use the Sobolev embedding
\mathbb{H}^{2}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{L}^{\infty}
and the
\mathbb{H}^{2}
regularity result instead.
This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Lemma 5.3
.

Assume that (
5.2a
) and (
5.2b
) hold for
s=1
.
For any
\epsilon\in(0,1)
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the approximate
solution
\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}
satisfies

(5.12)

\left\|{\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\Delta\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{\epsilon^{\beta+1}},

where
\beta
is defined by (
5.9
).

Proof.

Choosing
\boldsymbol{v}=-\Delta\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)\in\mathcal{S}_{n}
in (
5.4a
)
and using Hölder’s inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|{\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\Delta\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad=-\kappa_{1}\left\langle\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t),\Delta\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\kappa_{2}\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t),\Delta\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)\times\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t),\Delta\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}-\kappa_{2}\mu\left\langle|\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t),\Delta\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\Big(\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\Big)\left\|{\Delta\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{c}{\epsilon}\Big(\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{4}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\left\|{\Delta\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Rearranging the above equation, and using (
5.3
) and Lemma
5.1
yield

\displaystyle\left\|{\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\Delta\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\epsilon}\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}+1+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{L}^{\infty})}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{L}^{\infty})}^{4}\right).

By (
5.10
) and (
5.11
), we have the required result.
∎

Lemma 5.4
.

Assume that (
5.2a
) and (
5.2b
) hold for
s=1
.
For any
\epsilon\in(0,1)
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and
\alpha
satisfying (
2.5
), the approximate
solution
\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}
satisfies

(5.13)

\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1+\alpha}}^{2}+\epsilon^{\beta+2}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1+\alpha}}^{2}\lesssim 1,

where
\beta
is defined by (
5.9
).

Proof.

By (
A.4
) in the case
d=2
, and (
A.5
) in the case
d=3
, we have for any
\alpha
satisfying (
2.5
),

\displaystyle\epsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1+\alpha}}^{2}+\epsilon^{\beta+2}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(s)}\right\|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{1+\alpha}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{-1+\alpha}}^{2}+\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{-1+\alpha}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\epsilon^{\beta+2}\left(\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(s)}\right\|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{-1+\alpha}}^{2}+\left\|{\Delta\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(s)}\right\|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{-1+\alpha}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\epsilon^{\beta+2}\left(\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\Delta\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)\lesssim 1,

where in the last step we used Lemma
5.1
,
5.2
, and
5.3
, as required.
∎

The results in Lemmas
5.1
,
5.2
,
5.3
,
5.4
imply that the solution
\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}
exist on
[0,T]
for any given
T>0
. They can be summarised as follows: if (
5.2a
) and (
5.2b
) hold for
s=1
, then we have the following estimates for any
\alpha
satisfying (
2.5
):

(5.14)

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{H}^{1})}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{T}(\mathbb{H}^{1+\alpha})}+\sqrt{\epsilon}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{H}^{1+\alpha})}+\sqrt{\epsilon}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{H}^{1}_{\Delta})}

\displaystyle\lesssim 1,

\displaystyle\epsilon^{(\beta+1)/2}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{H}^{1})}+\epsilon^{(\beta+2)/2}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{H}^{1+\alpha})}+\epsilon^{(\beta+2)/2}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{H}^{1}_{\Delta})}

\displaystyle\lesssim 1.

Further technical estimates are shown in Appendix
B
, which yields the following result (see (
B.11
)): if (
5.2a
) and (
5.2b
) hold for
s=2
, then for any
\alpha
satisfying (
2.5
):

(5.15)

\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T^{\ast}}(\mathbb{L}^{2})}+\sqrt{\epsilon}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T^{\ast}}(\mathbb{H}^{1})}+\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{T^{\ast}}(\mathbb{H}^{1})}\lesssim 1.

We prove the existence of a global solution
\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}
to (
1.2
) in the following proposition.

Theorem 5.5
.

Let
\epsilon>0
and
T>0
be fixed. Let
T^{\ast}
be defined by (
3.8
).

(i)

If (
5.2a
) and (
5.2b
) hold for
s=1
, then there exists
a global strong solution
\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}
to (
1.2
) in the sense of Definition
2.1
. Furthermore, for any
\alpha
satisfying (
2.5
), there exists
a positive constant
c
independent of
\epsilon

satisfying

(5.16)

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{H}^{1})}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{T}(\mathbb{H}^{1+\alpha})}+\sqrt{\epsilon}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{H}^{1+\alpha})}+\sqrt{\epsilon}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{H}^{1}_{\Delta})}

\displaystyle\leq c,

\displaystyle\epsilon^{(\beta+1)/2}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{H}^{1})}+\epsilon^{(\beta+2)/2}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{H}^{1+\alpha})}+\epsilon^{(\beta+2)/2}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{H}^{1}_{\Delta})}

\displaystyle\leq c.

The constant
\beta
is defined by (
5.9
). Furthermore,
\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}\in C^{0,\widetilde{\gamma}}([0,T];\mathbb{H}^{1+\alpha})
for every
\widetilde{\gamma}\in[0,1)
.

(ii)

If (
5.2a
) and (
5.2b
) hold for
s=2
, then for any
\alpha
satisfying (
2.5
), we have

(5.17)

\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T^{\ast}}(\mathbb{L}^{2})}+\sqrt{\epsilon}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T^{\ast}}(\mathbb{H}^{1})}+\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{T^{\ast}}(\mathbb{H}^{1})}\lesssim 1.

Proof.

Fix
\epsilon>0
.
It follows from (
5.14
) that there exist

\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}\in W^{1,\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{H}^{1+\alpha})
and a subsequence
of
\{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
,
which we do not relabel, satisfying

(5.18)

\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}_{n}\to\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}\;\;\text{weakly}^{\ast}\text{ in $W^{1,\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{H}^{1+\alpha})$ as $n\to\infty$},\\

where the convergence is not uniform with respect to
\epsilon
.
Since the embeddings
\mathbb{H}^{1+\alpha}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{H}^{1}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{L}^{4}
are compact, it follows from the
Aubin–Lions lemma and (
5.18
) that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}-\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{T}(\mathbb{L}^{4})}=0\quad\text{and}\quad\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}^{\epsilon}-\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{T}(\mathbb{H}^{1})}=0.

The rest of the proof follows the same argument as in Theorem
3.6
. Estimates (
5.16
) and (
5.17
) follow from (
5.14
) and (
5.15
), respectively.
∎

We conclude this section by showing the stability of the strong solution
\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}
of (
1.2
) with respect to initial data, which implies uniqueness.

Theorem 5.6
.

Let
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}^{\epsilon},\boldsymbol{v}_{0}^{\epsilon}\in\mathbb{H}^{1}_{\Delta}
.
Let
\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}
and
\boldsymbol{v}^{\epsilon}
be the global strong solutions
associated with the initial data
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}^{\epsilon}

and
\boldsymbol{v}_{0}^{\epsilon}
, respectively, as conferred by
Theorem
5.5
. Then

\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}-\boldsymbol{v}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{H}^{1})}\lesssim\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}^{\epsilon}-\boldsymbol{v}_{0}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}_{\Delta}},

where the constant may depend on
\epsilon
and
T
. This implies
uniqueness of the solution
\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}
.

Proof.

Let
\boldsymbol{w}_{0}^{\epsilon}:=\boldsymbol{u}_{0}^{\epsilon}-\boldsymbol{v}_{0}^{\epsilon}

and
\boldsymbol{w}^{\epsilon}:=\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}-\boldsymbol{v}^{\epsilon}
.
Then it follows from (
2.6
) that, for any
\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\mathbb{H}^{1}
and
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{w}^{\epsilon}(t),\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\epsilon\left\langle\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{w}^{\epsilon}(t),\nabla\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\kappa_{1}\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{w}^{\epsilon}(t),\nabla\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{w}^{\epsilon}(t)\times\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(t),\nabla\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{v}^{\epsilon}(t)\times\nabla\boldsymbol{w}^{\epsilon}(t),\nabla\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

(5.19)

\displaystyle\quad+\kappa_{2}\left\langle\boldsymbol{w}^{\epsilon}(t),\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\kappa_{2}\mu\left\langle|\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(t)|^{2}\boldsymbol{w}^{\epsilon}(t)+(|\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(t)|^{2}-|\boldsymbol{v}^{\epsilon}(t)|^{2})\boldsymbol{v}^{\epsilon}(t),\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}=0.

Putting
\boldsymbol{\phi}=2\boldsymbol{w}^{\epsilon}
in (
5.19
) and
integrating we obtain, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{w}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+2\kappa_{1}\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}^{\epsilon}(s)\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+2\kappa_{2}\int_{0}^{t}\|\boldsymbol{w}^{\epsilon}(s)\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s+2\kappa_{2}\mu\int_{0}^{t}\||\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s)||\boldsymbol{w}^{\epsilon}(s)|\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}_{0}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-2\gamma\int_{0}^{t}\left\langle\boldsymbol{w}^{\epsilon}(s)\times\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s),\nabla\boldsymbol{w}^{\epsilon}(s)\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s-2\kappa_{2}\mu\int_{0}^{t}\left\langle(|\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s)|^{2}-|\boldsymbol{v}^{\epsilon}(s)|^{2})\boldsymbol{v}^{\epsilon}(s),\boldsymbol{w}^{\epsilon}(s)\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\mathrm{d}s.

The last two terms on the right-hand side can be estimated as
follows. For the middle term, by Lemma
A.3
we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{t}\left\langle\boldsymbol{w}^{\epsilon}(s)\times\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s),\nabla\boldsymbol{w}^{\epsilon}(s)\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\Phi(\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s))\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}^{\epsilon}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\delta\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{w}^{\epsilon}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s

for any
\delta>0
, where
\Phi
is defined in (
A.6
).
For the last term, we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{t}\left\langle(|\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s)|^{2}-|\boldsymbol{v}^{\epsilon}(s)|^{2})\boldsymbol{v}^{\epsilon}(s),\boldsymbol{w}^{\epsilon}(s)\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s)+\boldsymbol{v}^{\epsilon}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}^{\epsilon}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}^{\epsilon}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}^{\epsilon}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s)+\boldsymbol{v}^{\epsilon}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}^{\epsilon}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}^{\epsilon}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\mathrm{d}s+\delta\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}^{\epsilon}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s

(5.20)

\displaystyle\leq c\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}^{\epsilon}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\delta\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}^{\epsilon}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s

for any
\delta>0
, where in the penultimate step we used Young’s
inequality and the Sobolev embedding
\mathbb{H}^{1}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{L}^{6}
, and
in the last step we used Theorem
5.5
.

Altogether, rearranging the terms and choosing
\delta>0
sufficiently small, we infer that

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{w}^{\epsilon}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}_{0}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\Phi(\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s))\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}^{\epsilon}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s.

Note that
\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}\in L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{H}^{1+\alpha})
for all
\alpha
satisfying (
2.5
) by
Theorem
5.5
. Thus, by the Sobolev embeddings
\mathbb{H}^{1+\alpha}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{W}^{1,2d}
for
d=1,2
and
\mathbb{H}^{2}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{W}^{1,6}
for
d=3
, we have

(5.21)

\int_{0}^{t}\Phi(\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s))\,\mathrm{d}s\leq c,

where
c
may depend on
\epsilon
.
The Gronwall inequality and (
5.21
) give the required result.
∎

5.2.
Convergence of
\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}
to
\boldsymbol{u}

Here, we show convergence of the strong solution of the
\epsilon
-LLBE to that of the LLBE as
\epsilon\to 0^{+}
.

Theorem 5.7
.

The following statements hold true.

(i)

Consider the case when
d\in\{1,2\}
. Assume that
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\in\mathbb{H}^{1}_{\Delta}

and
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}^{\epsilon}
is chosen such that (
5.2a
) holds for
s=1
.
Let
\boldsymbol{u}
and
\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}
be global strong
solutions of (
1.1
) and (
1.2
),
respectively. Then for any
t\in[0,T]
,

(5.22)

\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(t)-\boldsymbol{u}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s)-\nabla\boldsymbol{u}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\lesssim\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}^{\epsilon}-\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}.

(ii)

If
d\in\{1,2,3\}
,
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\in\mathbb{H}^{2}_{\Delta}
, and
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}^{\epsilon}
is
chosen to satisfy (
5.2a
) for
s=2
, then for any
t\in[0,T^{\ast}]
,

(5.23)

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(t)-\boldsymbol{u}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s)-\Delta\boldsymbol{u}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\lesssim\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}^{\epsilon}-\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}.

The constants in the inequalities are independent of
\epsilon
.

Proof.

Let
\boldsymbol{v}=\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}-\boldsymbol{u}

and
\boldsymbol{v}_{0}=\boldsymbol{u}_{0}^{\epsilon}-\boldsymbol{u}_{0}
. We first prove
(i)
for the case
d\in\{1,2\}
.
By Definition
2.1
, for any
\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\mathbb{H}^{1}
and
t\in[0,T]
we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{v}(t),\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\kappa_{1}\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{v}(t),\nabla\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{v}(t)\times\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(t),\nabla\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}(t)\times\nabla\boldsymbol{v},\nabla\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

(5.24)

\displaystyle\;+\kappa_{2}\left\langle\boldsymbol{v}(t),\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\kappa_{2}\mu\left\langle|\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(t)|^{2}\boldsymbol{v}(t)+(|\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(t)|^{2}-|\boldsymbol{u}(t)|^{2})\boldsymbol{u}(t),\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}=-\epsilon\left\langle\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(t),\nabla\boldsymbol{\phi}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}.

Putting
\boldsymbol{\phi}=\boldsymbol{v}
in (
5.24
) and integrating yield, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\kappa_{1}\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{v}(s)\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s+\kappa_{2}\int_{0}^{t}\|\boldsymbol{v}(s)\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s+\kappa_{2}\mu\int_{0}^{t}\||\boldsymbol{u}(s)||\boldsymbol{v}(s)|\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{0}\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\left\langle\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s),\nabla\boldsymbol{v}(s)\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big|+\gamma\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\left\langle\boldsymbol{v}(s)\times\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s),\nabla\boldsymbol{v}(s)\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big|

(5.25)

\displaystyle\quad+\kappa_{2}\mu\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\left\langle(|\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s)|^{2}-|\boldsymbol{u}(s)|^{2})\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s),\boldsymbol{v}(s)\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big|.

We now estimate each term on the right hand side of (
5.25
).
The first term can be estimated by using Young’s inequality as follows:

(5.26)

\epsilon\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\left\langle\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s),\nabla\boldsymbol{v}(s)\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big|\leq\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{\kappa_{1}}\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s+\frac{\kappa_{1}}{4}\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{v}(s)\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s.

For the second term, by using Lemma
A.3
we obtain

(5.27)

\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\left\langle\boldsymbol{v}(s)\times\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s),\nabla\boldsymbol{v}(s)\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big|\leq\int_{0}^{t}\Phi(\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s))\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\frac{\kappa_{1}}{4}\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{v}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s.

For the last term, by the same argument as in (
5.20
) we have

(5.28)

\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\left\langle(|\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s)|^{2}-|\boldsymbol{u}(s)|^{2})\boldsymbol{u}(s),\boldsymbol{v}(s)\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\mathrm{d}s\Big|\leq c\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\frac{1}{4}\min\{\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}\}\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s,

where the constant
c
is independent of
\epsilon
by (
5.16
). Altogether, (
5.25
), (
5.26
), (
5.27
),
and (
5.28
) yield

\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{v}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq c\|\boldsymbol{v}_{0}\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+c\epsilon^{2}+c\int_{0}^{t}\big(1+\Phi(\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s))\big)\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s,

where we also noted that
\left\|{\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{T}(\mathbb{L}^{2})}
can be bounded by a constant independent of

\epsilon
by (
B.10
). Applying Gronwall’s inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{v}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}^{\epsilon}-\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\right)\exp\left(c\int_{0}^{t}\big(1+\Phi(\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s))\big)\mathrm{d}s\right)

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}^{\epsilon}-\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\right),

where we used (
B.5
) for
d=1,2
in the last step. The constant
c
is
independent of
\epsilon
, thus proving
(i)
.

Next, we prove
(ii)
. Repeating the above argument, we obtain the estimate (
5.22
) on
[0,T^{\ast}]
for
d=3
.
Putting
\boldsymbol{\phi}=-\Delta\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{v}}
in (
5.24
),
integrating, and noting (
A.1
) we obtain, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\kappa_{1}\int_{0}^{t}\|\Delta\boldsymbol{v}(s)\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s+\kappa_{2}\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{v}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{v}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\left\langle\Delta\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s),\Delta\boldsymbol{v}(s)\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\Big|\,\mathrm{d}s+\gamma\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\left\langle\boldsymbol{v}(s)\times\Delta\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s),\Delta\boldsymbol{v}(s)\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\Big|\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\kappa_{2}\mu\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\left\langle|\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s)|^{2}\boldsymbol{v}(s),\Delta\boldsymbol{v}(s)\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\Big|\,\mathrm{d}s

(5.29)

\displaystyle\quad+\kappa_{2}\mu\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\left\langle(|\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s)|^{2}-|\boldsymbol{u}(s)|^{2})\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s),\Delta\boldsymbol{v}(s)\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\Big|\,\mathrm{d}s.

The second term on the right-hand side can be estimated by using Young’s
inequality and (
B.11
) as follows:

\displaystyle\epsilon\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\left\langle\Delta\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s),\Delta\boldsymbol{v}(s)\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\Big|\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{\kappa_{1}}\int_{0}^{t}\|\Delta\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s)\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s+\frac{\kappa_{1}}{5}\int_{0}^{t}\|\Delta\boldsymbol{v}(s)\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq c\epsilon^{2}+\frac{\kappa_{1}}{5}\int_{0}^{t}\|\Delta\boldsymbol{v}(s)\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s.

For the third term, applying integration by parts and Young’s inequality we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\left\langle\boldsymbol{v}(s)\times\Delta\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s),\Delta\boldsymbol{v}(s)\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\Big|\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{v}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\frac{\kappa_{1}}{5}\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{v}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s,

where in the last step we used the Sobolev embedding
\mathbb{H}^{1}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{L}^{4}
.
For the second last term, it is easy to see that

\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\left\langle|\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s)|^{2}\boldsymbol{v}(s),\Delta\boldsymbol{v}(s)\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\Big|\,\mathrm{d}s\leq c\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\frac{\kappa_{1}}{5}\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{v}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s.

For the last term, by the same argument as in (
5.20
) we have

(5.30)

\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\left\langle(|\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s)|^{2}-|\boldsymbol{u}(s)|^{2})\boldsymbol{u}(s),\Delta\boldsymbol{v}(s)\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\mathrm{d}s\Big|\leq c\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\frac{\kappa_{1}}{5}\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{v}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s,

where the constant
c
is independent of
\epsilon
by (
5.16
).
Altogether, (
5.22
), (
5.29
)–(
5.30
)
yield

\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{v}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq c\|\boldsymbol{v}_{0}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+c\epsilon^{2}+c\int_{0}^{t}\big(1+\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\big)\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s.

Applying Gronwall’s inequality and using (
B.11
), we
obtain (
5.23
), completing the proof.
∎

6.
A fully-discrete finite element approximation for the
\epsilon
-LLBE

Throughout this section, we assume that
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}^{\epsilon}\in\mathbb{H}^{1}_{\Delta}
. Under this assumption, Theorem
5.5
guarantees the existence of a unique global strong solution
\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}
of the
\epsilon
-LLBE with regularity

(6.1)

\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}\in W^{1,\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{H}^{1+\alpha}),

where
\alpha
satisfies (
2.5
).

A linear finite element scheme for numerically solving the
\epsilon
-LLBE
can now be proposed in the following algorithm. To discretise in time, let
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
k=T/N
. We
partition
[0,T]
into
N
uniform subintervals with nodes
t_{n}=kn
for
n=0,1,2,\ldots,N
.

Algorithm 6.1

(Linear FEM for the
\epsilon
-LLBE)
.

Let
h>0
and
k>0
be given.

Input
: Given
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(0)}=P_{h}\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(0)\in\mathbb{V}_{h}
.

For

j=1
to
N

do
: Find
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(j)}\in\mathbb{V}_{h}
such that

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathrm{d}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(j)},\boldsymbol{\phi}_{h}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle+\epsilon\left\langle\nabla\mathrm{d}_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(j)},\nabla\boldsymbol{\phi}_{h}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\kappa_{1}\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(j)},\nabla\boldsymbol{\phi}_{h}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(j-1)}\times\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(j)},\nabla\boldsymbol{\phi}_{h}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

(6.2)

\displaystyle+\kappa_{2}\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(j)},\boldsymbol{\phi}_{h}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\kappa_{2}\mu\left\langle|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(j-1)}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(j)},\boldsymbol{\phi}_{h}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}=0,\quad\forall\boldsymbol{\phi}_{h}\in\mathbb{V}_{h},

Output
: a sequence of finite element functions
\big\{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(j)}\big\}_{j=1}^{N}
.

The above scheme is well-defined by the Lax–Milgram theorem.
Stability of the approximate solution in the
\ell^{\infty}(0,T;\mathbb{H}^{1})
norm, which holds unconditionally for an arbitrary number of iterations
n
, is shown in the following lemma.

Lemma 6.2
.

For any
n=1,2,\ldots,N
, there holds

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(n)}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\epsilon\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(n)}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+2k\sum_{j=1}^{n}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(j)}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\sum_{j=1}^{n}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(j)}-\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(j-1)}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\leq\|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(0)}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\epsilon\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(0)}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}.

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of Lemma
4.2
.
∎

We are now ready to prove the error estimate for the scheme (
6.2
). As before, for
j=1,\ldots,N
, letting
\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\epsilon,(j)}:=\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(t_{j})-P_{h}\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(t_{j})
and
\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}:=P_{h}\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(t_{j})-\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(j)}
, we decompose the error into

(6.3)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(t_{j})-\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(j)}=\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\epsilon,(j)}+\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}.

The following theorem shows an
a priori
error estimate for the numerical scheme given by Algorithm
6.1
. By using interpolation in the temporal variable, an approximate solution
\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}_{h,k}
can be defined from
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(n)}
such that
\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}_{h,k}-\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathbb{H}^{1})}
satisfies (
6.4
).

Theorem 6.3
.

Let
\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}
denote the strong solution of the
\epsilon
-LLBE with initial data
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}^{\epsilon}\in\mathbb{H}^{1}_{\Delta}
, and let
\big\{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(j)}\big\}_{j=1}^{N}
be the sequence generated by Algorithm
6.1
. Assume that
k\ell_{h}\lesssim 1
, where
\ell_{h}
is defined in (
4.5
).
Then for any
n=1,\ldots,N
, where
t_{n}\in[0,T]
, and
\alpha
satisfying (
2.5
), we have

(6.4)

\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(n)}-\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(t_{n})}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}\leq ce^{cT}(h^{\alpha}+k),

where the positive constant
c
is independent of
n
,
h
, or
k
, but may depend on
\epsilon^{-1}
.

Proof.

For any
\boldsymbol{\phi}_{h}\in\mathbb{V}_{h}
, the exact solution
\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}

of (
1.2
) satisfies, for any
\boldsymbol{\phi}_{h}\in\mathbb{V}_{h}
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(t_{j})-\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(t_{j-1}),\boldsymbol{\phi}_{h}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\epsilon\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(t_{j})-\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(t_{j-1}),\nabla\boldsymbol{\phi}_{h}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\int_{t_{j-1}}^{t_{j}}\kappa_{1}\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s),\nabla\boldsymbol{\phi}_{h}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\mathrm{d}s

(6.5)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{t_{j-1}}^{t_{j}}\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s)\times\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s),\nabla\boldsymbol{\phi}_{h}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{t_{j-1}}^{t_{j}}\kappa_{2}\left\langle(1+\mu|\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s)|^{2})\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s),\boldsymbol{\phi}_{h}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\mathrm{d}s=0.

Subtracting (
6.5
) from (
6.2
), noting (
4.17
) and (
4.2
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j-1)},\boldsymbol{\phi}_{h}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle+\epsilon\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}-\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j-1)},\nabla\boldsymbol{\phi}_{h}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\epsilon\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\epsilon,(j)}-\nabla\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\epsilon,(j-1)},\nabla\boldsymbol{\phi}_{h}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle+k\kappa_{1}\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}+\nabla\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\epsilon,(j)},\nabla\boldsymbol{\phi}_{h}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\kappa_{1}\int_{t_{j-1}}^{t_{j}}\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(t_{j})-\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s),\nabla\boldsymbol{\phi}_{h}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle+k\kappa_{2}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)},\boldsymbol{\phi}_{h}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\int_{t_{j-1}}^{t_{j}}\kappa_{2}\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(t_{j})-\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s),\boldsymbol{\phi}_{h}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle+\gamma H_{1}(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(j)},\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon},\boldsymbol{\phi}_{h})+\kappa_{2}\mu H_{2}(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(j)},\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon},\boldsymbol{\phi}_{h})

(6.6)

\displaystyle+k\kappa_{2}\mu\left\langle|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(j-1)}|^{2}\big(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}+\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\epsilon,(j)}\big),\boldsymbol{\phi}_{h}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}=0,

where
H_{1}
and
H_{2}
are defined in Lemma
4.3
.

To show (
6.4
), we will proceed inductively. Let
T>0
be given. Let
\ell_{h}
be as defined in (
4.5
) and suppose that
h
and
k
are sufficiently small such that

(6.7)

k\ell_{h}\leq\frac{c_{\infty}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{L}^{\infty})}}{c_{\mathrm{i}}e^{cT}\left(1+3c_{\infty}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{L}^{\infty})}\right)}\quad\text{and}\quad h^{\alpha}\ell_{h}\leq\frac{c_{\infty}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{L}^{\infty})}}{c_{\mathrm{i}}e^{cT}\left(1+3c_{\infty}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{L}^{\infty})}\right)},

where
c_{\infty}
and
c_{\mathrm{i}}
are constants in (
4.3
) for
p=\infty
and in (
4.6
), respectively.
In other words, we impose a mild time-step restriction
k\ell_{h}\lesssim 1
.
Let
M:=3c_{\infty}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{L}^{\infty})}
. For the inductive step, we assume that

(6.8)

\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}\leq M,\quad\text{for }j=0,1,\ldots,n-1.

Assuming this, we will show that

(6.9)

\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(n)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}\leq e^{cT}(1+M)(h^{\alpha}+k),\quad\text{and}\quad\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(n)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}\leq M,

where
c
is a constant independent of
n
,
h
, and
k
.

To this end, putting
\boldsymbol{\phi}_{h}=\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}
in (
6.6
), using the vector
identity (
4.8
), and rearranging the terms, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\left(\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}-\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+k\kappa_{1}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+k\kappa_{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\kappa_{2}\mu k\left\|{\big|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(j-1)}\big|\big|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}\big|}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=-k\kappa_{1}\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\epsilon,(j)},\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}-\epsilon\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\epsilon,(j)}-\nabla\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\epsilon,(j-1)},\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{t_{j-1}}^{t_{j}}\kappa_{1}\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(t_{j})-\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s),\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\mathrm{d}s-\int_{t_{j-1}}^{t_{j}}\kappa_{2}\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(t_{j})-\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s),\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad-\gamma H_{1}(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(j)},\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)})+\kappa_{2}\mu H_{2}(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(j)},\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)})-k\kappa_{2}\mu\left\langle|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(j-1)}|^{2}\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\epsilon,(j)},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\Big(ck\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\epsilon,(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\epsilon\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\epsilon,(j)}-\nabla\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\epsilon,(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+c\int_{t_{j-1}}^{t_{j}}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(t_{j})-\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\mathrm{d}s\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+c\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\int_{t_{j-1}}^{t_{j}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(t_{j})-\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+c\left|{H_{1}(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(j)},\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)})}\right|+c\left|{H_{2}(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(j)},\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)})}\right|+ck\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\epsilon,(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

(6.10)

\displaystyle=:R_{1}+\cdots+R_{5}.

We now estimate each term on the right-hand side. Let
\delta>0
be given and let
\alpha
satisfies (
2.5
). For
the first term, by (
4.18
) and Young’s inequality (noting the regularity (
6.1
)),

\displaystyle R_{1}

\displaystyle=\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\Big(ck\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\epsilon,(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\epsilon\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\epsilon,(j)}-\nabla\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\epsilon,(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+c\int_{t_{j-1}}^{t_{j}}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(t_{j})-\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\mathrm{d}s\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\Big(ck\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\epsilon,(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\epsilon k\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{L}^{2})}+ck^{2}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{L}^{2})}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\delta k\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+ck\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\epsilon,(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+ck\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{L}^{2})}^{2}+ck^{3}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{L}^{2})}^{2}

(6.11)

\displaystyle\leq ck(h^{2\alpha}+k^{2})+\delta k\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

where in the last step we used (
4.16
).
For the second term, by Hölder’s and Young’s inequalities, and (
4.18
), we have

\displaystyle R_{2}

\displaystyle=c\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\int_{t_{j-1}}^{t_{j}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(t_{j})-\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq ck^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{L}^{2})}\leq\delta k\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+ck^{3}.

For the term
R_{3}
, we invoke Lemma
4.3
(with
\boldsymbol{v}^{\epsilon,(j)}=\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(j)}
,

\boldsymbol{w}=\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}
,
\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\epsilon,(j)}=\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\epsilon,(j)}
,
and
\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\epsilon,(j)}=\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}
), use the fact that
\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}
is a strong solution, and apply (
4.16
) to obtain

\displaystyle R_{3}=c\,\big|H_{1}(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(j)},\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)})\big|

\displaystyle\leq c(1+M)k(h^{2\alpha}+k^{2})+ck\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+ck^{3}

(6.12)

\displaystyle\quad+\delta k\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+4\delta k\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

where we used (
6.8
) to
obtain the boundedness of
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(j)}
.
Similarly,

\displaystyle R_{4}

\displaystyle=c\,\big|H_{2}(\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(j)},\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)})\big|

\displaystyle\leq ckh^{2(1+\alpha)}+ck\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+ck^{3}+2\delta k\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\delta k\left\|{|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(j-1)}||\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}|}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

where we also used Lemma
6.2
. The constant
c
may depend on
\epsilon^{-1}
.
Finally,

\displaystyle R_{5}

\displaystyle=ck\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\epsilon,(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq ck\left\|{\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\epsilon,(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\delta k\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\leq ckh^{2(1+\alpha)}+\delta k\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}.

Altogether, we derive from (
6.10
) that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\Big(\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\Big)+\frac{1}{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\Big(\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\Big)+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}-\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+k\kappa_{1}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+k\kappa_{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\kappa_{2}\mu k\left\|{\big|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(j-1)}\big|\big|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}\big|}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq c(1+M)k(h^{2\alpha}+k^{2})+ck\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\delta k\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

(6.13)

\displaystyle\quad+8\delta k\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+4\delta k\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\delta k\left\|{|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(j-1)}||\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}|}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

We now choose
\delta=\frac{1}{10}\min\{\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\kappa_{2}\mu\}
to absorb the last four terms on the right-hand side to the last three terms on the left-hand side. In this manner we obtain

\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+(\epsilon+\kappa_{1})\Big(\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\Big)\leq c(1+M)k(h^{2\alpha}+k^{2})+ck\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j-1)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Summing this over
j=1,\ldots,n
, we deduce that

\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(n)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\leq c(1+M)(h^{2\alpha}+k^{2})+ck\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(j)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

where
c
possibly depends on
\epsilon^{-1}
, but is independent of
n
,
h
, and
k
.
Applying the discrete version of the Gronwall inequality
(
Sugiyama, 1969
, Lemma 1)
, we
deduce that

\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(n)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}\leq e^{cT}(1+M)(h^{\alpha}+k),

which is the first inequality in (
6.9
). This implies by (
4.6
) and (
4.3
),

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(n)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}^{\epsilon,(n)}-P_{h}\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(t_{n})}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}+\left\|{P_{h}\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}(t_{n})}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq c_{\mathrm{i}}\ell_{h}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\epsilon,(n)}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}+c_{\infty}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{L}^{\infty})}

\displaystyle\leq c_{\mathrm{i}}e^{cT}(1+M)\ell_{h}(h^{\alpha}+k)+c_{\infty}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\mathbb{L}^{\infty})}\leq M,

where in the last step we used (
6.7
). This proves (
6.9
), thus completing the induction argument.
Using (
4.16
)
and the triangle inequality, we obtain the first inequality in (
6.9
).
∎

As stated in the following theorem, it is possible to prove an unconditional
a priori
estimate in convex domains, assuming a higher regularity condition on the exact solution. Such regularity can be assured for initial data
